I mean, I still… I still do the things I’ve always done, and some of it’s okay. Maybe it’s just a little more muted than it used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, I still care about the same things, I still try to keep up with them. Maybe I’m not as… excited or involved as I used to be sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not guilty like I’ve done something terrible, exactly. It’s more… I get down on myself. I compare myself to the kind of man my dad was, and I feel like I should be further along or handling things better than I do. Nobody’s really saying that to me, but I hear it in my own head, if that makes sense. I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that. I mean, I get down on myself, but ending my life isn’t something I’ve really thought about doing. I hope that’s answering you clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower or faster than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not the quickest guy in the room anyway, but I haven’t really noticed myself moving unusually slow or sped up or anything. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: No, not really… nothing significant that I’ve noticed. My weight’s gone up and down a little like anybody’s, I guess, but I don’t think there’s been any big change over the last few years. At least not enough that I’d think to mention it—does that answer it okay?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do — getting through my routes, showing up for my volunteer shifts and all that — so it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything. But most days there’s this heaviness there, like I’m kind of just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this low feeling?\n\nPatient: Probably about three years now. It kind of started after my father passed away… I mean, I figured that was normal, and maybe it is, but it just never really lifted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things as much as you used to, or has that changed over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I enjoy things the same way, exactly… but I don’t know, maybe that’s just getting older too. I still do things, and I try not to make a big deal out of it. It’s more like things don’t always hit me the way they used to, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasure in, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there’s anything I’d say I get no pleasure from anymore. I mean, I still… I still do the things I’ve always done, and some of it’s okay. Maybe it’s just a little more muted than it used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, I still care about the same things, I still try to keep up with them. Maybe I’m not as… excited or involved as I used to be sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not guilty like I’ve done something terrible, exactly. It’s more… I get down on myself. I compare myself to the kind of man my dad was, and I feel like I should be further along or handling things better than I do. Nobody’s really saying that to me, but I hear it in my own head, if that makes sense. I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that. I mean, I get down on myself, but ending my life isn’t something I’ve really thought about doing. I hope that’s answering you clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower or faster than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not the quickest guy in the room anyway, but I haven’t really noticed myself moving unusually slow or sped up or anything. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: No, not really… nothing significant that I’ve noticed. My weight’s gone up and down a little like anybody’s, I guess, but I don’t think there’s been any big change over the last few years. At least not enough that I’d think to mention it—does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess, like anyone does, but I don’t think I’m anxious all the time or anything like that. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… it’s kind of been there most of the time for about three years, since my father died. Some days are better than others, sure, but I don’t think I’ve had a whole couple months where I felt like it was gone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never heard voices or seen things like that, at least not that I can think of. I mean, sometimes I might be tired and think I heard something, but nothing that seemed… real or unusual. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But most days there’s this heaviness there, like I’m kind of just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this low feeling?\n\nPatient: Probably about three years now. It kind of started after my father passed away… I mean, I figured that was normal, and maybe it is, but it just never really lifted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things as much as you used to, or has that changed over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I enjoy things the same way, exactly… but I don’t know, maybe that’s just getting older too. I still do things, and I try not to make a big deal out of it. It’s more like things don’t always hit me the way they used to, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasure in, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there’s anything I’d say I get no pleasure from anymore. I mean, I still… I still do the things I’ve always done, and some of it’s okay. Maybe it’s just a little more muted than it used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, I still care about the same things, I still try to keep up with them. Maybe I’m not as… excited or involved as I used to be sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not guilty like I’ve done something terrible, exactly. It’s more… I get down on myself. I compare myself to the kind of man my dad was, and I feel like I should be further along or handling things better than I do. Nobody’s really saying that to me, but I hear it in my own head, if that makes sense. I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that. I mean, I get down on myself, but ending my life isn’t something I’ve really thought about doing. I hope that’s answering you clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower or faster than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not the quickest guy in the room anyway, but I haven’t really noticed myself moving unusually slow or sped up or anything. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: No, not really… nothing significant that I’ve noticed. My weight’s gone up and down a little like anybody’s, I guess, but I don’t think there’s been any big change over the last few years. At least not enough that I’d think to mention it—does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess, like anyone does, but I don’t think I’m anxious all the time or anything like that. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… it’s kind of been there most of the time for about three years, since my father died. Some days are better than others, sure, but I don’t think I’ve had a whole couple months where I felt like it was gone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never heard voices or seen things like that, at least not that I can think of. I mean, sometimes I might be tired and think I heard something, but nothing that seemed… real or unusual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, or had trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might shift around if I’ve been sitting a long time, but I wouldn’t say I feel restless or like I can’t sit still. I don’t think that’s been a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess maybe a little, but I don’t know if it’s more than just getting older or working. I still get through what I need to do most days, so… I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in the number of activities you participate in or the frequency of participating in them over the past three years?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still doing the main things. I show up for work, and I still go to the pantry, so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But I guess it takes more pushing to get myself there than it used to.\n\nThere are some things I’ve kind of let sit, too. Like I started an application for a logistics supervisor position, but I haven’t finished it. I just keep thinking I’m not sure I could keep up with it, so… maybe that counts. I don’t know if I’d call it significant, but it has changed some.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I get down on myself, but ending my life isn’t something I’ve really thought about doing. I hope that’s answering you clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower or faster than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not the quickest guy in the room anyway, but I haven’t really noticed myself moving unusually slow or sped up or anything. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: No, not really… nothing significant that I’ve noticed. My weight’s gone up and down a little like anybody’s, I guess, but I don’t think there’s been any big change over the last few years. At least not enough that I’d think to mention it—does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess, like anyone does, but I don’t think I’m anxious all the time or anything like that. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… it’s kind of been there most of the time for about three years, since my father died. Some days are better than others, sure, but I don’t think I’ve had a whole couple months where I felt like it was gone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never heard voices or seen things like that, at least not that I can think of. I mean, sometimes I might be tired and think I heard something, but nothing that seemed… real or unusual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, or had trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might shift around if I’ve been sitting a long time, but I wouldn’t say I feel restless or like I can’t sit still. I don’t think that’s been a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess maybe a little, but I don’t know if it’s more than just getting older or working. I still get through what I need to do most days, so… I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in the number of activities you participate in or the frequency of participating in them over the past three years?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still doing the main things. I show up for work, and I still go to the pantry, so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But I guess it takes more pushing to get myself there than it used to.\n\nThere are some things I’ve kind of let sit, too. Like I started an application for a logistics supervisor position, but I haven’t finished it. I just keep thinking I’m not sure I could keep up with it, so… maybe that counts. I don’t know if I’d call it significant, but it has changed some.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt worn down or like I wanted a break from everything, but not like wishing I was dead. I wouldn’t say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a time in the past three years where your low feeling was significantly worse for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like there was one big breakdown or anything, but there have been stretches where that low feeling was heavier than usual for a couple weeks or more.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, honestly. I still get through my routes and I still show up for my volunteer shifts, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’m not functioning. But most days there’s this heavy, low feeling just sitting on me, like I’m going through the motions more than really living my own life, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. But most days there’s this heavy, low feeling just sitting on me, like I’m going through the motions more than really living my own life, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than you should be at times?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed that, exactly. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess I came in because I’ve just had this low feeling for a long time. I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do — getting through my routes, showing up for my volunteer shifts and all that — so it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything. But most days there’s this heaviness there, like I’m kind of just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this low feeling?\n\nPatient: Probably about three years now. It kind of started after my father passed away… I mean, I figured that was normal, and maybe it is, but it just never really lifted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things as much as you used to, or has that changed over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I enjoy things the same way, exactly… but I don’t know, maybe that’s just getting older too. I still do things, and I try not to make a big deal out of it. It’s more like things don’t always hit me the way they used to, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasure in, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there’s anything I’d say I get no pleasure from anymore. I mean, I still… I still do the things I’ve always done, and some of it’s okay. Maybe it’s just a little more muted than it used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, I still care about the same things, I still try to keep up with them. Maybe I’m not as… excited or involved as I used to be sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not guilty like I’ve done something terrible, exactly. It’s more… I get down on myself. I compare myself to the kind of man my dad was, and I feel like I should be further along or handling things better than I do. Nobody’s really saying that to me, but I hear it in my own head, if that makes sense. I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that. I mean, I get down on myself, but ending my life isn’t something I’ve really thought about doing. I hope that’s answering you clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower or faster than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not the quickest guy in the room anyway, but I haven’t really noticed myself moving unusually slow or sped up or anything. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: No, not really… nothing significant that I’ve noticed. My weight’s gone up and down a little like anybody’s, I guess, but I don’t think there’s been any big change over the last few years. At least not enough that I’d think to mention it—does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess, like anyone does, but I don’t think I’m anxious all the time or anything like that. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… it’s kind of been there most of the time for about three years, since my father died. Some days are better than others, sure, but I don’t think I’ve had a whole couple months where I felt like it was gone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never heard voices or seen things like that, at least not that I can think of. I mean, sometimes I might be tired and think I heard something, but nothing that seemed… real or unusual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, or had trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might shift around if I’ve been sitting a long time, but I wouldn’t say I feel restless or like I can’t sit still. I don’t think that’s been a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess maybe a little, but I don’t know if it’s more than just getting older or working. I still get through what I need to do most days, so… I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in the number of activities you participate in or the frequency of participating in them over the past three years?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still doing the main things. Like I started an application for a logistics supervisor position, but I haven’t finished it. I just keep thinking I’m not sure I could keep up with it, so… maybe that counts. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess I came in because I’ve just had this low feeling for a long time. I’m still doing what I’m supposed to do — getting through my routes, showing up for my volunteer shifts and all that — so it’s not like I’m falling apart or anything. But most days there’s this heaviness there, like I’m kind of just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this low feeling?\n\nPatient: Probably about three years now. It kind of started after my father passed away… I mean, I figured that was normal, and maybe it is, but it just never really lifted.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things as much as you used to, or has that changed over the past three years?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say I enjoy things the same way, exactly… but I don’t know, maybe that’s just getting older too. I still do things, and I try not to make a big deal out of it. It’s more like things don’t always hit me the way they used to, if that makes sense. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to find pleasure in, but no longer do?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there’s anything I’d say I get no pleasure from anymore. I mean, I still… I still do the things I’ve always done, and some of it’s okay. Maybe it’s just a little more muted than it used to be, but I wouldn’t want to overstate it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to care about?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’ve lost interest, exactly. I mean, I still care about the same things, I still try to keep up with them. Maybe I’m not as… excited or involved as I used to be sometimes, but I wouldn’t say I’ve stopped caring. I don’t know if that answers it the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty about things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, not guilty like I’ve done something terrible, exactly. It’s more… I get down on myself. I compare myself to the kind of man my dad was, and I feel like I should be further along or handling things better than I do. Nobody’s really saying that to me, but I hear it in my own head, if that makes sense. I don’t know if that counts as guilt or just being hard on myself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like that. I mean, I get down on myself, but ending my life isn’t something I’ve really thought about doing. I hope that’s answering you clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving slower or faster than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m probably not the quickest guy in the room anyway, but I haven’t really noticed myself moving unusually slow or sped up or anything. If other people have noticed, they haven’t said so. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past three years?\n\nPatient: No, not really… nothing significant that I’ve noticed. My weight’s gone up and down a little like anybody’s, I guess, but I don’t think there’s been any big change over the last few years. At least not enough that I’d think to mention it—does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say often, no. I mean, I worry about normal stuff, I guess, like anyone does, but I don’t think I’m anxious all the time or anything like that. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean… it’s kind of been there most of the time for about three years, since my father died. Some days are better than others, sure, but I don’t think I’ve had a whole couple months where I felt like it was gone. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I’ve never heard voices or seen things like that, at least not that I can think of. I mean, sometimes I might be tired and think I heard something, but nothing that seemed… real or unusual. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or fidgety, or had trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I might shift around if I’ve been sitting a long time, but I wouldn’t say I feel restless or like I can’t sit still. I don’t think that’s been a big issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have less energy than you used to?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I guess maybe a little, but I don’t know if it’s more than just getting older or working. I still get through what I need to do most days, so… I wouldn’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a significant decrease in the number of activities you participate in or the frequency of participating in them over the past three years?\n\nPatient: A little, yeah… I mean, I’m still doing the main things. I show up for work, and I still go to the pantry, so it’s not like I’ve stopped everything. But I guess it takes more pushing to get myself there than it used to.\n\nThere are some things I’ve kind of let sit, too. Like I started an application for a logistics supervisor position, but I haven’t finished it. I just keep thinking I’m not sure I could keep up with it, so… maybe that counts. I don’t know if I’d call it significant, but it has changed some.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt worn down or like I wanted a break from everything, but not like wishing I was dead. I wouldn’t say that.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a time in the past three years where your low feeling was significantly worse for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so.
